Chemical-Pace names what is being held, namely the resting burn rate of the whole body, which can fall by as much as thirty percent when thyroid hormone is absent.

Setting names the mechanism exactly, because these cells hold a set point for thyroid hormone and adjust their output to defend it, as a household thermostat defends a temperature.

Neuron marks it as a secretory cell whose set point is movable, since when the fat hormone leptin falls during fasting these cells reduce their output and the body deliberately turns its own heating down.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

A thyrotropin-releasing hormone neuron, shortened to TRH neuron, sets the body's metabolic rate.

Thyrotropin-releasing hormone is a chain of only three amino acids, the shortest peptide hormone known, released into the portal blood of the pituitary stalk, where it makes pituitary cells release thyroid stimulating hormone, which orders the thyroid gland in the neck to release thyroid hormone that sets the resting speed of nearly every cell in the body.

Crucially it is not a simple thermostat, because it also reads whether you have enough body fat to afford a fast metabolism and turns the throttle down when you do not.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

Small round or oval cell body with two to four sparsely branched dendrites and a very fine unmyelinated axon, scattered as a loose sheet through several subdivisions of the paraventricular nucleus rather than packed into a ball.

Its vesicles hold a large precursor protein, pro-thyrotropin-releasing hormone, snipped into five or six copies of the three-amino-acid hormone plus several other active fragments.

SIZE:

Cell bodies are roughly 10 to 15 micrometres across, dendritic trees span 100 to 250 micrometres, and the axon runs only 1 to 3 millimetres to the median eminence.

The hormone itself weighs about 362 daltons, small enough to slip easily through the windowed portal capillary wall.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

The hypophysiotropic TRH neurons, in the medial and caudal parvocellular paraventricular nucleus, control the thyroid axis and are the type described here.

Non-hypophysiotropic TRH neurons elsewhere in the same nucleus project into the brain and influence arousal, feeding and autonomic tone instead, while much larger TRH populations in the brainstem raphe, spinal cord and pancreas are counted with their own regions.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Hypophysiotropic cell bodies lie in the medial parvocellular subdivision of the paraventricular nucleus, with contributions from its periventricular and anterior parvocellular parts, and their axons end in the external zone of the median eminence.

Two interfaces define this cell: the median eminence, where terminals meet portal blood outside the blood-brain barrier, and the third ventricle wall, where tanycyte cell bodies sit and send processes down to wrap those very terminals.

A third interface matters functionally, the arcuate nucleus, whose appetite-sensing neurons project directly onto TRH dendrites and hand over the body's energy report.

NUMBER:

Human counts of hypophysiotropic TRH neurons have not been reliably published, so the honest answer is that the exact number is unknown.

A defensible bound, scaling from rodent counts of roughly 1,500 to 2,000 such cells per side and allowing for the larger human nucleus, is 5 to 15 thousand in total across both sides, with low confidence.

That would be roughly 0.1 percent of the hypothalamus and about one neuron in every 8 million in the nervous system, meaning a population smaller than a village runs the metabolism of an entire body.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Modest spontaneous firing at 1 to 6 spikes per second, irregular rather than bursting, with a low-threshold calcium current allowing rebound spikes when inhibition lifts and a hyperpolarisation-activated current pulling the membrane back toward threshold afterwards.

They are directly sensitive to their own feedback, since thyroid hormone changes the channels they express over hours to days, and they are also glucose-sensing and leptin-sensitive, shifting firing within minutes when fuel status changes.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

The dominant inputs come from the arcuate nucleus as two opposing messages onto the same dendrites.

Alpha-melanocyte stimulating hormone and cocaine- and amphetamine-regulated transcript, released by well-fed arcuate neurons, switch the TRH gene on, while agouti-related protein and neuropeptide Y from hungry arcuate neurons switch it off, which is why starvation lowers metabolic rate.

Further input arrives as noradrenaline from the brainstem carrying cold signals from the skin, glutamate from the dorsomedial nucleus, and GABA from the peri-paraventricular zone.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Very fine at 0.2 to 0.5 micrometres, unmyelinated, only 1 to 3 millimetres long, ending as boutons in the external zone of the median eminence.

Terminals are separated from portal blood by two basement membranes and physically embraced by tanycyte processes, so the axon lacks unconditional access to blood and the glia decide how much gets through.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Thyrotropin-releasing hormone is the defining product, acting on the TRH receptor of pituitary thyrotroph and lactotroph cells.

Many of these neurons also make glutamate, subsets co-express cocaine- and amphetamine-regulated transcript, neurotensin, cholecystokinin, enkephalin or corticotropin-releasing hormone, and the precursor protein yields further active peptides, so one gene produces a small family of messengers.

MYELINATION:

Not applicable in the usual sense, because these axons are unmyelinated and no myelinated variant exists.

They are only a few millimetres long and their output is a hormone level in blood, so insulation would give no speed benefit and would block the terminals from the vessels they must reach.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Low at baseline, since the peptide is three amino acids and the axon is short.

Demand rises sharply in cold exposure and untreated thyroid failure, when transcription of the precursor gene climbs steeply, and substrate is never limiting because the terminals lie in well-perfused tissue outside the blood-brain barrier.

PLASTICITY:

The most striking plasticity is not in the neuron but at its terminal, where tanycyte processes advance to engulf the endings or retreat to expose them, changing hormone delivery within hours with no change in firing.

The neuron itself alters its peptide output over days in response to thyroid hormone, cold and fasting, and leptin, released by fat tissue, is required for normal responses, which is why weight loss produces a persistently lowered metabolic set point.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Tanycytes are the specialised glia here and they do three things no other glia do for any other neuron.

They convert inactive thyroid hormone into its active form using an enzyme called type 2 deiodinase, creating a local pool of feedback signal made by glia rather than delivered by blood.

They carry pyroglutamyl peptidase II, a membrane enzyme that destroys thyrotropin-releasing hormone just outside the terminal, in an amount itself set by thyroid hormone, so the glia run a second independent feedback loop, and they also physically wrap and unwrap the terminals.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove this cell type and the paraventricular nucleus loses its metabolic throttle while keeping stress, water and autonomic control.

The result is central hypothyroidism, with slow heartbeat, cold intolerance, weight gain, constipation, dry skin, memory failure and profound fatigue, in a person whose thyroid gland is perfectly healthy and simply never told what to do, and the nucleus would also lose the link between fat stores and metabolic rate that lets the body economise during famine.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

The most interesting computation in this system happens outside the neuron, because the feedback signal that switches the TRH gene off is made by a glial cell.

Tanycytes convert thyroxine into the active triiodothyronine that then enters the neuron and acts on its nucleus, so the set point is a joint property of neuron and glia, and a second glial loop degrades the hormone after release with an enzyme whose level is thyroid-hormone-controlled.

The neuron performs genomic computation, since thyroid hormone receptors bind the precursor gene directly and change transcription over hours, and it integrates thyroid hormone, leptin and cold into one output.

It can even suppress its own gene despite low thyroid hormone during starvation or serious illness, a state called non-thyroidal illness syndrome that looks like a bug and is probably a survival feature, with retrograde endocannabinoid signalling, volume transmission and structural remodelling of arcuate synapses completing the picture.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS.

◦ Free triiodothyronine reaching the cell nucleus, largely made locally by tanycytes, 3 to 7 picomoles per litre equivalent.

◦ Leptin from fat tissue, 1 to 30 nanograms per millilitre.

◦ Alpha-melanocyte stimulating hormone and agouti-related protein drive from arcuate nucleus, 0 to 1 normalised each.

◦ Cold signal as noradrenaline from brainstem, 0 to 1 normalised.

• OUTPUTS.

◦ Thyrotropin-releasing hormone into portal blood, picomolar to nanomolar there.

◦ Glutamate and co-peptides at brain-directed collaterals.

• STATE.

◦ Membrane potential, internal calcium, precursor messenger RNA, releasable peptide pool, tanycyte gate openness, tanycyte degrading enzyme level.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Resting 1 to 6 spikes per second, cell body 10 to 15 micrometres, axon 1 to 3 millimetres, hormone about 362 daltons, feedback steep enough to hold free thyroxine within about 30 percent of set point.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Membrane 15 milliseconds, portal washout 6 seconds, thyroid stimulating hormone response 15 to 60 minutes, thyroid hormone response 6 to 48 hours, genomic feedback 12 to 72 hours.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Loss or suppression causes central hypothyroidism, in which thyroid hormone is low but thyroid stimulating hormone is not raised, the signature that the fault is in the brain and not the gland, while in starvation and severe illness the cells suppress their own gene despite low thyroid hormone, producing non-thyroidal illness syndrome.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to compute how much thyrotropin-releasing hormone actually reaches the pituitary, given thyroid hormone feedback, body fat signals, appetite signals and the degrading enzyme carried by tanycytes.
Create a number variable called free triiodothyronine with initial value 4.5.
Create a number variable called leptin with initial value 10.
Create a number variable called melanocyte stimulating drive with initial value 1.
Create a number variable called agouti related drive with initial value 0.1.
Create a number variable called enzyme activity with initial value 0.6.
Create a number variable called ratio with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called feedback with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called energy permission with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called arcuate balance with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called synthesis with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called delivered with initial value 0.
Set ratio to the quotient of free triiodothyronine divided by 4.5.
Set feedback to the product of ratio and the product of ratio and the product of ratio and ratio.
Set feedback to the quotient of 1 divided by the sum of 1 and feedback.
Set energy permission to the quotient of leptin divided by the sum of leptin and 4.
Set energy permission to the sum of 0.25 and the product of 0.75 and energy permission.
Set arcuate balance to the difference of the product of 0.6 and melanocyte stimulating drive and the product of 0.6 and agouti related drive.
Set arcuate balance to the sum of 1 and arcuate balance.
If arcuate balance is less than 0.1, then perform the following actions.
    Set arcuate balance to 0.1.
End the conditional block.
Set synthesis to the product of feedback and the product of energy permission and arcuate balance.
Set delivered to the quotient of synthesis divided by the sum of 1 and enzyme activity.
Display synthesis to the console.
Display delivered to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because the interesting result is a comparison across four body states, and a table of tuples plus one formatted print makes that comparison immediate.

# How thyroid hormone, body fat and the tanycyte enzyme set TRH output.
def trh_output(free_t3, leptin, alpha_msh, agrp, enzyme):
    """Return (synthesis, delivered) in arbitrary units per minute."""
    feedback = 1.0 / (1.0 + (free_t3 / 4.5) ** 4)      # T3 shuts the gene down
    energy = 0.25 + 0.75 * (leptin / (leptin + 4.0))   # leptin permits it
    arcuate = max(0.1, 1.0 + 0.6 * alpha_msh - 0.6 * agrp)  # fed versus hungry
    synthesis = feedback * energy * arcuate
    delivered = synthesis / (1.0 + enzyme)             # tanycyte enzyme degrades
    return synthesis, delivered
states = [
    # name,                free T3, leptin, alphaMSH, AgRP, enzyme
    ("rested and well fed",    4.5,   10.0,      1.0,  0.1,  0.6),
    ("three days fasting",     3.2,    1.0,      0.2,  0.9,  0.6),
    ("untreated overactive",   9.0,   10.0,      1.0,  0.1,  1.4),
    ("primary hypothyroid",    1.5,   10.0,      1.0,  0.1,  0.3),
]
for name, t3, lep, msh, agrp, enz in states:
    made, sent = trh_output(t3, lep, msh, agrp, enz)
    print("%-22s synthesis %.3f  delivered %.3f" % (name, made, sent))
# The fasting row is the point: thyroid hormone is low, which should raise TRH,
# yet output falls. That paradox is the non-thyroidal illness syndrome.

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

It is the only neuron whose feedback signal is manufactured for it by a glial cell and whose released hormone is destroyed by an enzyme that the same glial cell carries on its surface.

Look for a small scattered cell in the medial parvocellular paraventricular nucleus contacted by both hunger and fullness fibres from the arcuate nucleus, whose three-amino-acid product sets the burn rate of the entire body.

CHAPTER 96. GONADOTROPIN-RELEASING HORMONE (GNRH) NEURONS (Fertility Rhythm-Setting Neuron) (NON-COG)

Every human puberty is switched on by about a thousand cells that were not born inside the brain.

The old label is a hormone barcode compressed to an acronym.

Fertility names the faculty, since without these cells puberty never begins and reproduction never happens.

Rhythm-Setting names the mechanism and corrects the usual misconception that the signal is a level, because it is a beat, and the pituitary responds only to pulses delivered roughly once an hour, a steady infusion of the same total amount shutting reproduction down instead of driving it.

Neuron marks it as one of only a few thousand such cells in the entire human brain, and the one neuron type not born in the brain at all, migrating in from the nose along olfactory nerves before birth.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

Gonadotropin-releasing hormone (GnRH) neurons are the final common pathway for reproduction, squirting a ten-amino-acid peptide (a very short protein-like messenger) into the private blood supply of the pituitary gland.

The squirt must be roughly hourly, because the pituitary answers pulses and goes deaf to a steady signal.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

The small spindle-shaped cell body sends out a hybrid process called a dendron, which takes synapses like a dendrite, fires its spike 30 to 140 micrometres out, and releases hormone like an axon.

SIZE:

The cell body is about 10 to 20 micrometres across, a fifth the width of a human hair, while the dendron runs one to two millimetres.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

Unipolar and bipolar forms both occur, bipolar dominating in adults, and fetal studies describe a second, cryptic population that migrates to the basal forebrain instead of the hypothalamus.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Cell bodies scatter through the medial preoptic area, septo-preoptic continuum, organum vasculosum of the lamina terminalis, anterior hypothalamus and infundibular (arcuate) nucleus.

Terminals occupy the external zone of the median eminence, the interface between brain tissue and the fenestrated (leaky) portal capillaries, where the blood-brain barrier is deliberately absent.

NUMBER:

The adult human hypothalamus holds roughly 1,000 to 2,000 of these cells, a figure repeated across surveys and held with moderate confidence.

That is 0.01 to 0.02 percent of the roughly 10 million hypothalamic neurons, and one cell in 43 million of the roughly 86 billion in the brain.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

At rest the inside sits near minus 60 millivolts, firing under one spike per second, and during a pulse the cell bursts for tens of seconds, with calcium from internal stores setting burst length.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

The pulse command comes from KNDy neurons of the infundibular nucleus, named for their kisspeptin, neurokinin B and dynorphin content, while glutamate and gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA) synapses land along the dendron, GABA unusually exciting these cells.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Thin, mostly unbranched and one to two millimetres long, ending in swellings packed with dense-core vesicles against portal capillaries, its slow conduction irrelevant to a hormone read in minutes.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

The product is GnRH, a decapeptide whose blocked front end and amidated tail slow its destruction in blood, and many of these cells also carry galanin.

MYELINATION:

Not Applicable, because these projections are unmyelinated: myelin is fatty insulation that speeds conduction, and a cell whose output is an hourly blood pulse gains nothing from it.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Firing is cheap but peptide manufacture is not, so the budget is dominated by protein synthesis and hauling vesicles two millimetres for every pulse.

PLASTICITY:

At puberty excitatory synapses increase over months, while across the cycle oestrogen remodels synapses and glial coverage within days, switching the cells from hourly pulses to the surge that triggers ovulation.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Beta-tanycytes, glia lining the third ventricle floor, wrap these terminals and hold them off the blood, then retract within hours under oestrogen and nitric oxide, so a glial cell is the tap on the reproductive axis.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Delete this cell type and the preoptic area still runs sleep, temperature and parenting, but the reproductive future vanishes: the gonads stay infantile and puberty never arrives.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

The dendron receives synapses and fires spikes in the same cable, so computation happens in the wire rather than at the cell body.

They synchronise without touching, through shared kisspeptin drive and through GnRH acting back on their own terminals by volume transmission, in which a messenger diffuses through tissue instead of crossing one synapse.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: kisspeptin drive as a 0 to 1 signal, glutamate and GABA currents of 0 to 50 picoamps, oestradiol of 50 to 1500 picomoles per litre acting indirectly.

• OUTPUTS: GnRH into portal blood, up to about 100 picograms per millilitre per pulse, reaching pituitary gonadotrophs only.

• STATE: membrane voltage, dendron trigger-zone voltage, calcium store, burst timer, vesicle pool, tanycyte coverage.

• PARAMETERS: rest minus 60 millivolts, threshold minus 45 millivolts, baseline under 1 hertz, burst 5 to 15 hertz, cell count 1,000 to 2,000.

• TIME CONSTANTS: membrane 20 milliseconds, burst 20 to 60 seconds, interpulse interval 60 minutes follicular and 90 luteal.

• FAILURE MODE: failed migration causes Kallmann syndrome, absent puberty with absent smell, while continuous rather than pulsatile exposure desensitises the pituitary, as leuprolide does deliberately.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to generate hourly gonadotropin-releasing hormone pulses from a kisspeptin pulse generator.
Create a number variable called brake with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called hormone with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called generator with initial value 1.
Create a number variable called step with initial value 0.
While step is less than 14400, repeat the following steps.
    If brake is greater than 4, then perform the following actions.
        Set generator to 0.
    Else if brake is less than 0.2, then perform the following actions.
        Set generator to 1.
    End the conditional block.
    Set brake to the sum of brake and the difference of the product of 0.067 and generator and the quotient of brake divided by 1200.
    Set hormone to the sum of hormone and the difference of the product of 0.01 and generator and the quotient of hormone divided by 300.
    Set step to the sum of step and 1.
End the loop.
Display hormone to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because the point is a slow feedback loop read at a glance, and it needs no libraries.

# KNDy pulse generator: a dynorphin brake gating one GnRH neuron, one second per step.
brake, hormone, gen = 0.0, 0.0, 1.0
for step in range(14400):                  # four simulated hours
    if brake > 4.0:                        # brake wins, burst ends
        gen = 0.0
    elif brake < 0.2:                      # brake cleared, next pulse starts
        gen = 1.0
    brake += 0.067 * gen - brake / 1200.0
    hormone += 0.01 * gen - hormone / 300.0
    if step % 300 == 0:
        print("t=%6.1f min  generator=%.0f  portal GnRH=%6.4f"
              % (step / 60.0, gen, hormone))

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

It is the only neuron in the body born outside the brain, in the nasal placode, crawling in along the olfactory nerves before birth.

That is why a failed journey leaves a patient who cannot smell and never enters puberty.

CHAPTER 97. GROWTH HORMONE-RELEASING HORMONE (GHRH) NEURONS (Growth-Spurt Prompting Neuron) (NON-COG)

Children grow in the dark, and these are the cells that decide it.

The old label is a hormone barcode compressed to an acronym.

Growth names the faculty, since the pituitary hormone these cells release drives the lengthening of bone and the building of tissue.

Spurt is the load-bearing word, because growth hormone is not secreted evenly but in sharp bursts, most of them during deep sleep, and the burst is what the tissues read.

Prompting rather than commanding is honest, since fewer than two thousand of these cells sit in the arcuate nucleus and their prompts are only permitted to become a pulse when the opposing brake lifts.

Neuron marks it as a small secretory population with an outsized effect on body size.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

Growth hormone-releasing hormone (GHRH) neurons make a 44-amino-acid peptide, cut from a 108-amino-acid precursor, and release it into the pituitary's private blood supply.

There it makes somatotroph cells release growth hormone in pulses, the loudest arriving 60 to 90 minutes after sleep onset.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

Small round to oval cells with two to four modest dendrites and one fine axon ending in boutons crammed with dense-core vesicles, the granules that store peptide hormone.

SIZE:

Cell bodies are about 10 to 18 micrometres across, and the axon is under half a millimetre because the median eminence lies directly beneath the nucleus.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

Roughly 60 percent answer circulating growth hormone with STAT5 phosphorylation, an internal mark that the receptor was engaged, while the rest appear blind to it.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Cell bodies fill the infundibular nucleus, the human equivalent of the rodent arcuate nucleus, with a scatter in the ventromedial and paraventricular regions.

Terminals lie in the external zone of the median eminence, the interface between brain tissue and the fenestrated portal capillaries, a deliberate window in the blood-brain barrier.

NUMBER:

Rodent counts put this population below 2,000 cells, and scaling to human arcuate size gives 5,000 to 15,000, held with low confidence because no human count exists.

Against the measured infundibular total of about 520,000 neurons, even the top estimate is under 0.15 percent of the ten million hypothalamic neurons.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Resting potential sits near minus 57 millivolts, and somatostatin, an inhibitor, paradoxically triggers rhythm by opening potassium channels, so that on withdrawal the cell rebounds into a burst.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Inhibition arrives from periventricular somatostatin and neuropeptide Y cells, excitation from ghrelin acting directly, and the output goes to blood rather than to a neuron.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Short, fine and unmyelinated, ending on the space around portal vessels, with conduction speed irrelevant to a hormone measured in minutes.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

GHRH is the defining product, released with neurotensin or galanin in subsets, and because release faces blood this is called neurosecretion rather than neurotransmission.

MYELINATION:

Not Applicable: these axons are unmyelinated, because the distance is a fraction of a millimetre and the readout is a slow blood hormone.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Peptide synthesis dominates and swings across the day, and the leaky median eminence next door gives direct access to circulating fuel.

PLASTICITY:

Growth hormone suppresses GHRH gene expression within hours, fasting and sleep loss reshape it over days, and puberty raises pulse amplitude several-fold.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Tanycytes, ependymal glia lining the third ventricle floor, sheath these terminals and gate their access to portal blood, a job ordinary astrocytes never do.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove this cell type and the nucleus still runs appetite, reproduction and prolactin, but stature goes: children stop growing, and adults gain fat while losing muscle and bone.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

This cell is a rhythm generator using inhibition as its metronome, so a plain integrate-and-fire model gives the wrong output.

It rewrites its own transcription under hormone feedback, senses blood ghrelin directly, couples to neighbours by collaterals, is gated by glial endfeet, and encodes frequency rather than amount.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: somatostatin tone 0 to 1, ghrelin up to 1,000 picograms per millilitre before meals, growth hormone 0 to 30 nanograms per millilitre, slow-wave-sleep flag.

• OUTPUTS: GHRH into portal blood, up to 500 picograms per millilitre, targeting pituitary somatotrophs only.

• STATE: membrane voltage, somatostatin-set potassium conductance, rebound timer, releasable GHRH pool, messenger RNA level.

• PARAMETERS: rest minus 57 millivolts, burst 5 to 20 hertz, cell count 5,000 to 15,000, growth hormone pulses 6 to 12 daily.

• TIME CONSTANTS: membrane 20 milliseconds, rebound tens of seconds, pool refill 6 minutes, hormone clearance 12 to 20 minutes, transcription hours.

• FAILURE MODE: GHRH receptor mutations cause growth hormone deficiency and severe short stature, while a tumour elsewhere secreting GHRH drives acromegaly, the disease of adult overgrowth.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to produce pulses of growth hormone from a GHRH neuron gated by somatostatin.
Create a number variable called pool with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called blood hormone with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called brake with initial value 0.2.
Create a number variable called step with initial value 0.
While step is less than 1440, repeat the following steps.
    Set excitation to 1.3.
    If step is less than 150, then perform the following actions.
        Set excitation to 2.8.
    End the conditional block.
    Set gate to the quotient of 1 divided by the sum of 1 and the product of 3 and brake.
    Set pool to the sum of pool and the difference of the product of excitation and gate and the quotient of pool divided by 6.
    Set blood hormone to the sum of blood hormone and the difference of the product of 2 and pool and the quotient of blood hormone divided by 12.
    Set brake to the sum of brake and the difference of the product of 0.01 and blood hormone and the quotient of brake divided by 90.
    Set step to the sum of step and 1.
End the loop.
Display blood hormone to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

C is chosen because this is a tight three-variable loop over thousands of steps, and C keeps the arithmetic explicit.

#include <stdio.h>
#include <math.h>

int main(void) {
    double ghrh = 0.0, gh = 0.0, sst = 0.2;   /* pool, blood hormone, brake */
    int m;
    for (m = 0; m < 1440; m++) {              /* one minute per step, 24 hours */
        double sws = (m < 150) ? 1.5 : 0.0;    /* first deep sleep block */
        double excite = 1.3 + sws + ((fmod(m, 300.0) < 30.0) ? 0.4 : 0.0);
        double gate = 1.0 / (1.0 + exp(3.0 * (sst - 1.0))); /* brake shuts the tap */
        ghrh += excite * gate - ghrh / 6.0;
        gh   += 2.0 * ghrh - gh / 12.0;
        sst  += 0.01 * gh - (sst - 0.2) / 90.0;             /* short loop feedback */
        if (m % 30 == 0)
            printf("t=%4d min GHRH=%7.3f GH=%8.3f SST=%7.3f\n", m, ghrh, gh, sst);
    }
    return 0;
}

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

No other neuron is silenced into rhythm: give this cell its inhibitor and it starts oscillating, the opposite of what inhibition does elsewhere.

And none signs a body so visibly, since its output is your height.

CHAPTER 98. SOMATOSTATIN NEURONS (Pulse-Trough Curbing Neuron) (NON-COG)

This is the cell that says no, and the body grows in the gaps between its refusals.

Somatostatin names the peptide and says nothing about the rhythm the cell exists to create.

Pulse-Trough names what the cell actually builds, because a pulse only exists if there are valleys between the peaks, and it is this cell that digs the valleys.

Curbing names the work, since the peptide it releases does nothing but silence the growth hormone signal, acting through potassium channels that quieten the prompting neurons directly.

Neuron marks it as the cell whose irregular suppression turns the chatter of the prompting cells into slow population-level oscillations, teaching the general lesson that in the body a rhythm is usually built by an inhibitor rather than by a driver.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

Somatostatin neurons of the periventricular zone release a small ring-shaped peptide into the pituitary blood supply to shut growth hormone off, so it escapes only when this brake lifts.

The same peptide also restrains thyroid-stimulating hormone, so one inhibitor polices two hormonal axes.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

Small fusiform cells with two or three sparse dendrites parallel to the third ventricle wall, sending axons to the median eminence and branches into the infundibular nucleus.

SIZE:

Cell bodies measure roughly 10 to 15 micrometres, among the smallest neuroendocrine cells in the hypothalamus, with axons of one to two millimetres.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

This entry covers the hypophysiotropic subtype that controls the pituitary; other somatostatin cells act locally in feeding circuits, and a vast unrelated family exists elsewhere.

WHERE IT LIVES:

The core population fills the periventricular nucleus and adjacent anterior periventricular area, a thin sheet pressed against the third ventricle above the optic chiasm.

Terminals sit in the external zone of the median eminence at the brain-to-blood interface, and collaterals cross into the infundibular nucleus, straddling two territories.

NUMBER:

A defensible working estimate is 10,000 to 25,000 human hypophysiotropic somatostatin neurons, held with low confidence because no direct human stereological count exists.

That is roughly 0.1 to 0.25 percent of the approximately 10 million neurons of the hypothalamus.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Resting potential near minus 60 millivolts with tonic firing of a few spikes per second, rising with growth hormone and falling during deep sleep.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Inputs are growth hormone feedback, glucose- and leptin-sensitive afferents, noradrenaline and sleep inhibition; outputs are peptide into blood and synapses onto GHRH neurons.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Thin unmyelinated axons of one to two millimetres end beside portal capillaries, plus a collateral making conventional synapses, so one cell both broadcasts and wires.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Two lengths of one peptide, somatostatin-14 and somatostatin-28, cut from a 116-amino-acid precursor, act on five receptor subtypes that open potassium channels and make targets harder to excite.

MYELINATION:

Not Applicable: these axons are unmyelinated, because the pathway is short and the signal is a slow blood concentration.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Modest, dominated by continuous peptide manufacture rather than electrical work, and eased by a leaky barrier nearby giving easy access to glucose.

PLASTICITY:

Growth hormone raises somatostatin gene expression over hours, sex steroids reshape the population across puberty, and chronic sleep loss blunts the nightly withdrawal over days.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Tanycyte endfeet cover these terminals and gate peptide release into blood; nothing unusual is reported for oligodendrocytes here, because there is no myelin to make.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Take this cell away and the zone still relays circadian and autonomic traffic, but growth hormone stops arriving in sharp nightly pulses and dribbles out continuously.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

This cell imposes a rhythm on its target rather than passing one along, because inhibiting a GHRH neuron and then releasing it produces a rebound burst.

It uses volume transmission, causes an immediate potassium-channel effect then a slower shift in its target's glutamate and GABA balance, and rewrites its own transcription.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: growth hormone 0 to 30 nanograms per millilitre, glucose 3 to 10 millimoles per litre, brainstem noradrenaline, and a slow-wave-sleep inhibition flag.

• OUTPUTS: somatostatin-14 and somatostatin-28 into portal blood, plus inhibition of GHRH neurons.

• STATE: membrane voltage, firing rate, somatostatin-14 pool, somatostatin-28 pool, fraction of target receptors still on the surface.

• PARAMETERS: rest minus 60 millivolts, baseline 2 to 6 hertz, stress up to 12 hertz, cell count 10,000 to 25,000.

• TIME CONSTANTS: membrane 20 milliseconds, somatostatin-14 clearance about 3 minutes, somatostatin-28 several times longer, receptor recovery 5 minutes.

• FAILURE MODE: too little brake permits continuous growth hormone output, which is why somatostatin copies such as octreotide and lanreotide treat acromegaly.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to compute how strongly a somatostatin neuron closes the growth hormone tap.
Create a number variable called short peptide with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called long peptide with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called receptors with initial value 1.
Create a number variable called step with initial value 0.
While step is less than 3600, repeat the following steps.
    Set firing to 4.
    If both step is greater than 1200 and step is less than 2400 are true, then perform the following actions.
        Set firing to 12.
    End the conditional block.
    Set short peptide to the sum of short peptide and the difference of the product of 0.02 and firing and the quotient of short peptide divided by 3.
    Set long peptide to the sum of long peptide and the difference of the product of 0.006 and firing and the quotient of long peptide divided by 15.
    Set total to the sum of short peptide and long peptide.
    Set occupancy to the quotient of total divided by the sum of total and 0.05.
    Set receptors to the difference of receptors and the product of 0.002 and occupancy.
    Set gate to the difference of 1 and the product of occupancy and receptors.
    Set step to the sum of step and 1.
End the loop.
Display gate to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

JavaScript is chosen because these are coupled leaky pools a reader can paste straight into a browser console.

// Periventricular somatostatin neuron closing the growth hormone tap.
let sst14 = 0.0, sst28 = 0.0;   // the two peptide lengths, arbitrary units
let receptors = 1.0;            // fraction of target receptors still on the surface
for (let s = 0; s < 3600; s++) {                        // one second per step
  const firing = (s > 1200 && s < 2400) ? 12.0 : 4.0;   // exercise raises the brake
  sst14 += 0.02 * firing - sst14 / 3.0;
  sst28 += 0.006 * firing - sst28 / 15.0;               // longer form clears slowly
  const total = sst14 + sst28;
  const occupancy = total / (total + 0.05);
  receptors += (1.0 - receptors) / 300.0 - 0.002 * occupancy;   // receptor fatigue
  const ghGate = 1.0 - occupancy * receptors;           // 1 = tap open, 0 = shut
  if (s % 600 === 0)
    console.log("t=" + s + "s SST14=" + sst14.toFixed(3) + " gate=" + ghGate.toFixed(3));
}

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

It is the only hypothalamic neuron whose whole job is subtraction, and whose most important moment is the moment it stops, every night, an hour after you fall asleep.

CHAPTER 99. TUBEROINFUNDIBULAR DOPAMINE (TIDA) NEURONS (Milk-Hormone Curbing Neuron) (NON-COG)

A few thousand cells stand between every human being and spontaneous lactation.

The old label is an anatomical route compressed to an acronym, naming where the axons run rather than what they hold back.

Milk-Hormone names the target in words a reader can picture, namely prolactin, the pituitary hormone that makes milk.

Curbing names the unusual arrangement and corrects an assumption, because this is the one pituitary hormone whose default state is on, held down continuously by dopamine from these cells rather than switched on by a releasing factor.

Neuron marks it as a cell whose restraint is dynamic rather than fixed, since prolactin feeds back onto it and during lactation its membrane oscillations shift to a faster pattern that reverses again after weaning, which is why suckling raises milk supply.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

Tuberoinfundibular dopamine (TIDA) neurons pour dopamine into the pituitary's blood supply, where it clamps down on prolactin, the hormone of milk production.

Prolactin alone among pituitary hormones is held back by an inhibitor rather than a releasing factor, so these cells work continuously.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

Small oval cells with two or three simple dendrites, packed into the dorsomedial arcuate nucleus as the A12 catecholamine group, and pigmented in adult humans.

SIZE:

Cell bodies are about 10 to 16 micrometres with axons under half a millimetre, so portal dopamine changes within seconds of a change in firing.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

Three related groups feed the pituitary: the tuberoinfundibular group ending in the median eminence, the tuberohypophysial group, and the periventricular hypophysial group.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Cell bodies occupy the dorsomedial arcuate (infundibular) nucleus of the mediobasal hypothalamus, beside the third ventricle.

Terminals lie in the external zone of the median eminence, the interface between neural tissue and fenestrated portal capillaries.

NUMBER:

A working estimate is 5,000 to 15,000 human tuberoinfundibular dopamine neurons, held with low confidence because published human counts are descriptive rather than stereological.

That is roughly 0.05 to 0.15 percent of the ten million hypothalamic neurons, far fewer than the substantia nigra yet controlling a whole endocrine axis.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

The signature is a slow rhythmic swing of membrane voltage giving alternating bursts and silences, reported in mouse slices at about 0.05 cycles per second.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Oxytocin excites these cells, opioids inhibit them, and prolactin is the main feedback, switching them from phasic bursting to tonic firing with broadened spikes.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Very short, fine, unmyelinated axons ending in varicosities, and they reportedly carry little dopamine transporter, so released dopamine is swept away by blood rather than recaptured.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Dopamine, made by tyrosine hydroxylase, acting on D2 receptors of pituitary lactotrophs, with many cells also releasing gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA).

MYELINATION:

Not Applicable: the axons are unmyelinated, because the journey is under half a millimetre and the endpoint is a blood vessel.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Dopamine synthesis is oxygen- and iron-dependent and unpackaged dopamine oxidises into reactive by-products, which is why these cells accumulate pigment.

PLASTICITY:

Prolactin acting through the STAT5 pathway raises tyrosine hydroxylase and dopamine output over tens of minutes, and lactation resets the whole network.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Tanycytes wrap these terminals and gate access to blood, and because they take up and metabolise monoamines they also act as a local dopamine sink.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Without this cell type the arcuate nucleus still handles appetite, growth and reproduction, but restraint over the breast is lost: milk appears in people not nursing.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

The slow oscillation is intrinsic, so this cell is a clock rather than a relay, and a simple threshold unit loses its most characteristic behaviour.

It switches firing mode under hormonal control, co-releases GABA onto neighbours, synchronises within a local network, and rewrites its own enzyme levels.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: prolactin, about 5 to 25 nanograms per millilitre at rest and hundreds during lactation, plus oxytocin, opioid peptides and local GABA and glutamate.

• OUTPUTS: dopamine into portal blood onto D2 receptors of lactotrophs, plus local GABA onto arcuate neighbours.

• STATE: membrane voltage, slow oscillator variable, tyrosine hydroxylase activity, dopamine pool, phasic-versus-tonic mode flag.

• PARAMETERS: rest minus 60 millivolts, oscillation about 0.05 hertz in rodent slices, within-burst firing 5 to 20 hertz, cell count 5,000 to 15,000.

• TIME CONSTANTS: membrane 20 milliseconds, oscillator 5 to 20 seconds, dopamine clearance seconds, prolactin feedback about 30 minutes, lactational resetting days.

• FAILURE MODE: antipsychotic blockade of D2 receptors removes the brake and causes hyperprolactinaemia with milk secretion and infertility, while prolactin-secreting tumours are treated with cabergoline.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to switch a tuberoinfundibular dopamine neuron from phasic to tonic firing under prolactin.
Create a number variable called voltage with initial value minus 60.
Create a number variable called slow current with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called dopamine with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called prolactin with initial value 5.
Create a number variable called step with initial value 0.
While step is less than 600000, repeat the following steps.
    If step is greater than 180000, then perform the following actions.
        Set prolactin to 60.
    End the conditional block.
    Set target to the difference of the sum of minus 45 and the product of 0.45 and prolactin and the product of 25 and slow current.
    Set voltage to the sum of voltage and the product of 0.02 and the difference of target and voltage.
    Set wanted to 0.
    If voltage is greater than minus 47, then perform the following actions.
        Set wanted to 1.
        Set dopamine to the sum of dopamine and 0.005.
    End the conditional block.
    Set slow current to the sum of slow current and the product of 0.002 and the difference of wanted and slow current.
    Set dopamine to the difference of dopamine and the quotient of dopamine divided by 6000.
    Set step to the sum of step and 1.
End the loop.
Display dopamine to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because the interesting result is a change of firing mode over minutes, and the oscillator and the slow hormone feedback sit side by side.

import math
# TIDA neuron: intrinsic slow oscillation, then prolactin switches it to tonic firing.
dt, v, h, th, da, prl = 0.01, -60.0, 0.0, 1.0, 0.0, 5.0
for step in range(600000):                   # 100 simulated minutes
    t = step * dt
    if t > 1800.0:
        prl = 60.0                           # suckling bout or tumour at 30 min
    th += dt * ((1.0 + 0.05 * prl) - th) / 1800.0          # STAT5 feedback, ~30 min
    v += dt * ((-45.0 + 0.45 * prl - 25.0 * h) - v) / 0.5  # fast membrane
    h += dt * (1.0 / (1.0 + math.exp(-(v + 47.0) / 3.0)) - h) / 5.0  # slow current
    da += dt * (0.5 * (1.0 if v > -47.0 else 0.0) * th - da / 60.0)
    if step % 30000 == 0:                    # every five simulated minutes
        print("t=%5.1f min V=%7.2f mV TH=%5.2f dopamine=%7.3f" % (t / 60.0, v, th, da))

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

It is the only major neuron whose normal state is to say no continuously, which is why a drug blocking its receptor can start lactation in a man.

CHAPTER 100. AGOUTI-RELATED PEPTIDE AND NEUROPEPTIDE Y (AGRP/NPY) NEURONS OF THE ARCUATE NUCLEUS (Hunger-Driving Foraging Neuron) (NON-COG)

Kill these cells in an adult mouse and it starves to death in about six days beside a full bowl of food.

The old label is two peptide barcodes bolted to an address, which teaches nothing about what the cell does.

Hunger names the sensation these cells create rather than merely report, since switching them on in a fed animal makes it eat within seconds and destroying them in an adult animal causes it to stop eating and starve.

Driving is literal, because their activity is a motivating urge and not a readout.

Foraging names the behaviour the urge produces, the searching that happens before any food is found, and this is where the peptide neuropeptide Y earns its place, sustaining the drive across the gap between leaving and eating.

Neuron marks it as an arcuate cell whose feeding effect disappears entirely if that peptide is deleted.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

Agouti-related peptide and neuropeptide Y (AgRP/NPY) neurons are the hunger cells, sitting where the blood-brain barrier leaks and tasting the blood for signs of an empty body.

Switching them on with light in a mouse produces voracious feeding within minutes, with no training required.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

Small oval to fusiform cells with two to four sparse dendrites, but with an unusually wide axonal broadcast and three messengers packed into one cell.

SIZE:

Cell bodies are roughly 10 to 15 micrometres across, while one axon tree reaches several millimetres into the paraventricular nucleus, lateral hypothalamus and brainstem.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

Individual cells target one or two nuclei rather than all, and in human tissue about 70 percent of neuropeptide Y neurons carry no agouti-related peptide.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Cell bodies fill the ventromedial arcuate (infundibular) nucleus, beside the third ventricle and directly above the median eminence, an interface between the sealed brain and the open bloodstream.

Terminals reach the paraventricular nucleus, lateral hypothalamic area, dorsomedial hypothalamus, bed nucleus of the stria terminalis, central amygdala, periaqueductal grey and parabrachial nucleus.

NUMBER:

Mouse counts put agouti-related peptide neurons near ten thousand, and scaling by arcuate size gives a human working estimate of 30,000 to 90,000, held with low confidence.

Against the measured human bound of about 520,000 infundibular neurons, that is 0.3 to 0.9 percent of the ten million hypothalamic neurons.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Resting potential near minus 55 millivolts with tonic firing of one to six spikes per second that rises on fasting, yet collapses within seconds of merely seeing or smelling food.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Leptin and insulin inhibit them while ghrelin excites them, and their critical output is the GABA synapse onto the parabrachial nucleus, whose loss alone causes starvation.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Fine, unmyelinated and extensively branched, with thousands of varicosities releasing on three timescales from millisecond GABA to hour-long peptide.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA) acts in milliseconds and neuropeptide Y over minutes, while agouti-related peptide acts over hours as an inverse agonist, pushing the melanocortin receptor below its resting activity.

MYELINATION:

Not Applicable: these axons are unmyelinated, because they carry a slow motivational state measured in minutes and hours.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Electrically cheap but committed to heavy peptide manufacture across a large axon tree, and directly sensitive to glucose, so they are sensors as much as neurons.

PLASTICITY:

Synapses onto these cells rearrange within hours of a change in leptin, among the fastest structural rewiring known in the adult brain.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Tanycytes shuttle leptin and ghrelin from blood into the nucleus and set how leaky the barrier is, while arcuate microglia turn reactive within days of a high-fat diet.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove this cell type and the arcuate nucleus still controls growth and reproduction, but urgency disappears: nothing reports that fuel is running out, and an animal starves beside food.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

One cell multiplexes three chemical channels whose timescales span six orders of magnitude, so a simulation needs at least three output variables.

It reads hormones straight from blood, spreads peptide by volume transmission, inhibits its antagonist neighbours, remodels its synapses, and generates a feeling the animal works to switch off.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: leptin 1 to 100 nanograms per millilitre, insulin, ghrelin up to 1,000 picograms per millilitre when fasting, glucose 3 to 10 millimoles per litre, food-seen flag.

• OUTPUTS: GABA onto the parabrachial nucleus and arcuate neighbours, neuropeptide Y onto Y1 and Y5 receptors, agouti-related peptide onto melanocortin 4 receptors.

• STATE: membrane voltage, firing rate, GABA pool, neuropeptide Y tone, agouti-related peptide tone, leptin signalling level.

• PARAMETERS: rest about minus 55 millivolts, baseline 1 to 6 hertz, fasted up to 20 hertz, cell count 30,000 to 90,000.

• TIME CONSTANTS: GABA 10 milliseconds, sensory shut-off 1 to 5 seconds, neuropeptide Y minutes, agouti-related peptide hours, remodelling hours to days.

• FAILURE MODE: losing these neurons in adulthood causes lethal loss of appetite, over-activity drives the hunger of leptin deficiency and post-diet regain, and their suppression contributes to cancer cachexia.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to turn hunger and fullness hormones into fast and slow output from an AgRP and NPY neuron.
Create a number variable called peptide y with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called agrp with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called step with initial value 0.
While step is less than 6000, repeat the following steps.
    Set drive to 1.4.
    If step is greater than 300, then perform the following actions.
        Set drive to 0.
    End the conditional block.
    Set firing to the quotient of the product of 20 and drive divided by the sum of 1 and drive.
    Set fast signal to the product of 0.05 and firing.
    Set peptide y to the sum of peptide y and the difference of the product of 0.001 and firing and the quotient of peptide y divided by 200.
    Set agrp to the sum of agrp and the difference of the product of 0.0002 and firing and the quotient of agrp divided by 6000.
    Set step to the sum of step and 1.
End the loop.
Display agrp to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Go is chosen because three output channels run on wildly different clocks in one loop, and Go's explicit types make those timescales impossible to overlook.

package main

import "fmt"

func main() {
	ghrelin, leptin := 1.0, 0.2 // high when empty, low when thin
	var fastGABA, npy, agrp float64
	dt := 0.1 // seconds per step
	for step := 0; step < 6000; step++ {
		sight := 0.0
		if float64(step)*dt >= 30.0 { // food comes into view at 30 seconds
			sight = 1.0
		}
		drive := 2.0*ghrelin - 3.0*leptin - 2.5*sight
		if drive < 0 {
			drive = 0
		}
		firing := 20.0 * drive / (1.0 + drive) // saturating, spikes per second
		fastGABA = 0.05 * firing               // milliseconds
		npy += dt * (0.01*firing - npy/20.0)   // tens of seconds
		agrp += dt * (0.002*firing - agrp/600.0)
		if step%600 == 0 {
			fmt.Printf("t=%5.1fs firing=%5.2fHz GABA=%5.3f NPY=%6.4f AgRP=%7.5f\n",
				float64(step)*dt, firing, fastGABA, npy, agrp)
		}
	}
}

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

Nothing else in the brain kills so quickly by absence, and nothing else falls silent at the mere sight of food, before a single calorie has been absorbed.

CHAPTER 101. PRO-OPIOMELANOCORTIN AND COCAINE- AND AMPHETAMINE-REGULATED TRANSCRIPT (POMC/CART) NEURONS OF THE ARCUATE NUCLEUS (Meal-Ending Fullness Neuron) (NON-COG)

One gene in this cell is chopped into a satiety hormone, a pigment hormone and the body's own morphine.

The old label is a pair of gene and transcript barcodes bolted to an address, which teaches nothing about what the cell does.

Meal-Ending names the work, because after eating it releases alpha-melanocyte-stimulating hormone, the cleavage product of the pro-opiomelanocortin protein, which suppresses further feeding.

Fullness names the state the cell reports, since it reads leptin and insulin from the bloodstream through the leaky blood-brain barrier at the base of the hypothalamus and fires when energy stores are adequate.

Neuron marks it as the direct opponent of the hunger-driving cells sitting beside it in the same nucleus, and losing pro-opiomelanocortin function produces severe early-onset human obesity.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

Pro-opiomelanocortin and cocaine- and amphetamine-regulated transcript (POMC/CART) neurons are the fullness cells, opponents of the hunger neurons beside them.

They read leptin and insulin as proof of fuel and answer with alpha-melanocyte-stimulating hormone, telling downstream cells to stop eating and burn more.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

Small oval cells with two to four modest dendrites, intermingled with the hunger neurons that inhibit them, carrying enzymes that cut one precursor into several finished hormones.

SIZE:

Cell bodies are about 12 to 18 micrometres, and axons range from a millimetre to tens of millimetres reaching the spinal cord.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

Some release glutamate and others GABA, leptin-receptor and insulin-receptor cells barely overlap, and a brainstem population handles meal-by-meal fullness.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Cell bodies occupy the ventrolateral and ventromedial arcuate (infundibular) nucleus, at the interface between the sealed brain and the leaky median eminence where blood leptin arrives.

Axons end in the paraventricular nucleus, lateral hypothalamic area, dorsomedial nucleus, periaqueductal grey, parabrachial nucleus and spinal intermediolateral column.

NUMBER:

Mouse counts range from three thousand to nine thousand, and scaling to human arcuate size gives 40,000 to 100,000 human cells, held with low confidence.

Against the measured human infundibular total of about 520,000 neurons, that is 0.4 to 1 percent of the ten million hypothalamic neurons.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Resting potential near minus 65 millivolts with steady firing of two to eight spikes per second, held low by a slow after-hyperpolarisation.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Leptin, insulin and serotonin excite them, GABA from neighbouring hunger neurons inhibits them, and their key output lands on melanocortin 4 receptor neurons of the paraventricular nucleus.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Thin, largely unmyelinated and widely branched, releasing peptide from varicosities along the axon rather than only at endings.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Alpha-melanocyte-stimulating hormone signals fullness, transcript peptide reinforces it, and co-released beta-endorphin, the body's own opioid, encourages eating.

MYELINATION:

Largely absent, and sparse where it appears, because the signal is a slow metabolic report rather than a time-critical command.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Precursor synthesis and enzymatic cutting are expensive, and these glucose-sensing cells are lost in rodents after prolonged high-fat feeding.

PLASTICITY:

Leptin rewires the synapses onto these cells within hours, and early-life leptin surges set their innervation of the paraventricular nucleus for life.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Tanycytes carry leptin into the nucleus, and their failure is one proposed mechanism of leptin resistance, while arcuate microglia turn reactive on a high-fat diet.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove this cell type and the nucleus still senses hunger, but there is no voice for enough: eating never stops, and obesity begins in infancy.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

Chemistry is the computation here, since the same messenger RNA yields different hormones depending on which cutting enzymes are present.

It co-releases an opioid that opposes its own satiety peptide, spreads peptide by volume transmission, remodels synapses under leptin, and can be destroyed by diet-driven inflammation.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: leptin 1 to 100 nanograms per millilitre, insulin, serotonin, GABA from agouti-related peptide neurons, glucose 3 to 10 millimoles per litre.

• OUTPUTS: alpha-melanocyte-stimulating hormone onto melanocortin 3 and 4 receptors, beta-endorphin onto mu-opioid receptors, and fast glutamate or GABA by subtype.

• STATE: membrane voltage, after-hyperpolarisation, satiety peptide pool, beta-endorphin pool, precursor stock, leptin signalling.

• PARAMETERS: rest minus 65 millivolts, threshold minus 45 millivolts, firing 2 to 8 hertz, cell count 40,000 to 100,000.

• TIME CONSTANTS: membrane 20 milliseconds, after-hyperpolarisation 300 milliseconds, satiety peptide clearance 2 minutes, beta-endorphin 10 minutes, leptin rewiring hours.

• FAILURE MODE: mutations in the precursor gene, convertase 1, the leptin receptor or the melanocortin 4 receptor cause severe childhood obesity, treatable with setmelanotide, approved in the United States in November 2020.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to turn leptin and insulin into a melanocortin fullness signal from a POMC and CART neuron.
Create a number variable called voltage with initial value minus 65.
Create a number variable called adaptation with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called satiety peptide with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called step with initial value 0.
While step is less than 60000, repeat the following steps.
    Set target to the difference of minus 37 and adaptation.
    Set voltage to the sum of voltage and the product of 0.05 and the difference of target and voltage.
    Set adaptation to the difference of adaptation and the quotient of adaptation divided by 300.
    If voltage is greater than or equal to minus 45, then perform the following actions.
        Set voltage to minus 60.
        Set adaptation to the sum of adaptation and 6.
        Set satiety peptide to the sum of satiety peptide and 0.0002.
    End the conditional block.
    Set satiety peptide to the difference of satiety peptide and the quotient of satiety peptide divided by 120000.
    Set step to the sum of step and 1.
End the loop.
Display satiety peptide to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Java is chosen because this cell needs an explicit spiking model with an adaptation current and separately clocked peptide pools.

public class PomcNeuron {
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        double leptin = 1.0, insulin = 0.6;   // arbitrary units of fuel reporting
        double agrpBlock = 0.3;               // AgRP holding MC4R below baseline
        double v = -65.0, adapt = 0.0;        // millivolts
        double aMsh = 0.0, bEnd = 0.0;        // peptide pools
        final double dt = 0.001, tauM = 0.020, tauA = 0.30;   // seconds
        for (int step = 0; step < 60000; step++) {
            double drive = 1.4 * leptin + 0.6 * insulin;
            double target = -65.0 + 14.0 * drive - adapt;
            v += dt / tauM * (target - v);
            adapt -= dt * adapt / tauA;
            double spike = 0.0;
            if (v >= -45.0) { v = -60.0; adapt += 6.0; spike = 1.0; }
            aMsh += dt * (0.2 * spike - aMsh / 120.0);
            bEnd += dt * (0.1 * spike - bEnd / 600.0);
            double mc4r = aMsh / (aMsh + 0.05) - agrpBlock;   // net fullness drive
            if (step % 15000 == 0)
                System.out.printf("t=%5.1fs aMSH=%6.4f MC4R=%6.3f%n", step * dt, aMsh, mc4r);
        }
    }
}

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

It is the only neuron whose single gene product is butchered inside the cell into a fullness hormone, a skin-darkening hormone and an opioid, and the only one with a drug built to replace its missing message.

CHAPTER 102. MELANOCORTIN RECEPTOR 4-EXPRESSING NEURONS OF THE PARAVENTRICULAR NUCLEUS (Appetite-Halting Hub Neuron) (NON-COG)

Roughly one person in every five hundred is born with a broken copy of a single gene, and the reason they are ravenous from their first year of life is that a few tens of thousands of these cells never hear the brain's order to stop eating.

The old name is a receptor barcode joined to a nucleus address.

Appetite-Halting names the proven job, because restoring melanocortin receptor 4 in this nucleus alone in receptor-deleted mice abolishes nearly all of the overeating, showing the cell is both necessary and sufficient to hold feeding in check.

Hub names its position, because it is the collection point where the fullness signal from the arcuate cells is received and passed on as glutamate to the parabrachial nucleus in the brainstem, which finally stops the meal.

Neuron marks it as the cell whose receptor carries the commonest single-gene cause of human obesity, at roughly one in a thousand people.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

These are small neurons packed into the paraventricular nucleus (PVN), a pair of wing-shaped cell clusters that sit one on each side of the third ventricle, the narrow fluid-filled slot running down the midline of the brain.

Each cell carries a docking protein on its surface called the melanocortin 4 receptor (MC4R), which is a lock that fits one key in particular.

The key is alpha-melanocyte-stimulating hormone (alpha-MSH), a short chain of amino acids released by nerve fibres arriving from the arcuate nucleus, a feeding-control centre sitting lower down in the same hypothalamus.

When alpha-MSH docks, these neurons become more excitable, and the animal eats less and burns more.

A rival peptide called agouti-related peptide (AgRP) docks at the same lock and does the opposite, so the cell is permanently caught in a tug of war between two chemical messages meaning "stop eating" and "keep eating".

The output of that tug of war is the single most powerful genetic determinant of human body weight that we know of.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

They are parvocellular neurons, meaning small-bodied, in contrast to the giant magnocellular neurons in the same nucleus that make the hormones oxytocin and vasopressin.

The cell body is oval or spindle-shaped, with three to five main dendrites, which are the branching input antennae of a neuron.

The dendrites are sparsely studded with spines, the tiny mushroom-shaped knobs where excitatory contacts land, which tells you these cells are tuned by relatively few, strong, hormone-modulated inputs rather than by thousands of weak ones.

Some of these dendrites run towards the ventricle wall, where they can sample the cerebrospinal fluid compartment for circulating signals.

SIZE:

The cell body is about 10 to 18 micrometres across, roughly one fifth the width of a human hair.

The dendritic field spans about 200 to 350 micrometres, so a single cell listens to a patch of tissue about a third of a millimetre wide.

The axon, the single output cable, can be long: a PVN neuron projecting to the brainstem or to the spinal cord runs 3 to 12 centimetres.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

At least three functionally distinct populations carry MC4R in the PVN.

• Neurons projecting to the lateral parabrachial nucleus in the brainstem, which set how much food is eaten in a meal.

• Neurons projecting to the nucleus of the solitary tract, the brainstem's gut-signal relay, which act on meal termination and nausea.

• Neurons descending to the intermediolateral cell column of the spinal cord, which set the sympathetic outflow to brown fat and so control heat production.

Overlapping with these are cells that also make oxytocin, thyrotropin-releasing hormone, or pituitary adenylate cyclase-activating polypeptide (PACAP), so the same melanocortin signal is translated into feeding, thyroid drive and stress-axis tone through different chemical channels.

WHERE IT LIVES:

The medial parvocellular, dorsal parvocellular and ventral subdivisions of the paraventricular nucleus of the hypothalamus, in both hemispheres.

A thinner population extends into the adjacent periventricular zone and the anterior hypothalamic area.

The important interface is the border between the PVN and the third ventricle, where the neurons abut tanycytes, the specialised stretched glial cells that line the ventricle and control what gets through from the fluid to the tissue.

A second interface matters just as much: the PVN sits directly downstream of the arcuate nucleus and the median eminence, the one part of the hypothalamus where the blood-brain barrier is deliberately leaky.

NUMBER:

The whole paraventricular nucleus holds about 85 thousand neurons in the human brain.

Rodent work suggests roughly a quarter of PVN neurons carry the MC4R gene, which gives a human estimate of about 20 thousand cells, with low confidence, because no unbiased stereological count of human MC4R neurons has been published.

The defensible bound is 10 thousand to 30 thousand.

That is about 0.2 percent of the roughly 10 million neurons of the hypothalamus, and about one neuron in every four million in the nervous system as a whole.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Resting membrane voltage, the steady electrical charge across the cell's skin when nothing is happening, sits at about minus 55 to minus 65 millivolts.

They fire spontaneously and slowly, typically 0.5 to 5 action potentials per second, an action potential being the brief electrical spike that carries a message down the axon.

Input resistance is high, often 500 million to 1 billion ohms, which means very small currents change the voltage a lot, so these cells are exquisitely sensitive to weak hormonal nudges.

The signature mechanism is unusual: alpha-MSH acting at MC4R closes a potassium channel called Kir7.1, and closing a channel that normally leaks positive charge out of the cell makes the cell more positive and more likely to fire.

AgRP opens the same channel, and it does so even without any alpha-MSH present, so the receptor has a resting activity that AgRP actively switches off.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Inputs come from arcuate proopiomelanocortin neurons carrying alpha-MSH, from arcuate AgRP neurons that also release the inhibitory transmitter gamma-aminobutyric acid, from the nucleus of the solitary tract carrying gut and vagal news, from the bed nucleus of the stria terminalis, and from the suprachiasmatic clock.

Outputs go to the lateral parabrachial nucleus, the nucleus of the solitary tract, the dorsal motor nucleus of the vagus, the ventrolateral periaqueductal grey and the spinal intermediolateral column.

A typical cell carries an estimated 2,000 to 6,000 synaptic contacts, and the mix of excitatory to inhibitory contacts on it is rearranged within hours by fasting or by the fat-derived hormone leptin.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Axons are thin, about 0.2 to 0.6 micrometres in diameter, and conduct slowly at roughly 0.5 to 2 metres per second.

They branch modestly, contacting two or three target nuclei from one parent cell, and they end in varicosities, bead-like swellings along the fibre from which peptide is released into the surrounding tissue rather than only into a tight synaptic cleft.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

The primary fast transmitter is glutamate, the brain's standard excitatory chemical, loaded into vesicles by vesicular glutamate transporter 2.

Glutamate release is not optional decoration: when MC4R was restored in these neurons in receptor-deficient mice, obesity was largely corrected, and blocking glutamate release from the very same cells abolished the benefit entirely.

Subsets co-release oxytocin, thyrotropin-releasing hormone, corticotropin-releasing hormone, PACAP or nesfatin-1.

MYELINATION:

Local collaterals inside the hypothalamus are unmyelinated.

Long descending axons to the brainstem and spinal cord acquire a thin myelin sheath, the fatty insulating wrap that speeds conduction, typically with only a few wraps, so conduction stays modest.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Firing slowly, these are not expensive cells, but they are unusually dependent on accurate metabolic reporting, and they are damaged by chronic overnutrition.

In diet-induced obesity the surrounding PVN loses neurons while the MC4R population itself survives, yet the surviving cells lose mitochondria, the cell's power plants, and lose MC4R protein from the surface.

PLASTICITY:

Synaptic inputs from the arcuate nucleus are remodelled within hours of a change in leptin or in feeding state.

Receptor trafficking is a second layer of plasticity: MC4R is escorted to the surface by a chaperone protein called melanocortin receptor accessory protein 2, and many disease-causing mutations act simply by leaving the receptor stranded inside the cell.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Astrocytes here are not passive: they carry leptin receptors, and their processes physically retract from synapses on these neurons within days of high-fat feeding, changing how much glutamate lingers.

Tanycytes at the ventricle wall shuttle leptin and other blood-borne signals into the nucleus and can be reconfigured by fasting.

Microglia, the brain's resident immune cells, multiply and become inflamed in the mediobasal hypothalamus within a day of high-fat feeding, before any body fat has been gained, which is one of the earliest known events in diet-induced obesity.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove this one cell type and the paraventricular nucleus is still a superb hormone factory and still commands the stress axis, but it becomes deaf to the body's fat stores.

The region would keep issuing orders about cortisol, water and heat while having no opinion at all about whether the meal should end, and the animal would eat itself into massive obesity with a normal stress response and normal water balance.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

The MC4R receptor changes the cell's excitability by directly gating the Kir7.1 potassium channel, apparently without the usual second-messenger cascade, which is a rare example of a hormone receptor working like a switch on a channel.

The receptor has constitutive activity, meaning it signals with no key in the lock at all, and AgRP is an inverse agonist that shuts that resting signal down, so the cell reports the ratio of two peptides rather than the presence of one.

Oxytocin-containing members of this population release peptide from their dendrites as well as their axon terminals, bathing neighbours in hormone by volume transmission, which is chemical signalling that spreads through the tissue instead of travelling down a wire.

These cells also instruct blood flow and sympathetic tone in distant fat tissue, so a synaptic event here becomes a change in whole-body heat production minutes later.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS.

◦ alpha-MSH concentration from arcuate proopiomelanocortin terminals, 0 to 1 normalised occupancy of MC4R.

◦ AgRP concentration from arcuate AgRP terminals, 0 to 1 normalised occupancy, acting as an inverse agonist.

◦ Fast glutamate and gamma-aminobutyric acid conductances from ascending brainstem and forebrain inputs, 0 to 20 nanosiemens.

◦ Circulating leptin, 1 to 40 nanograms per millilitre, acting indirectly through the arcuate nucleus and directly on local astrocytes.

• OUTPUTS.

◦ Glutamate release onto lateral parabrachial nucleus, nucleus of the solitary tract and spinal intermediolateral column.

◦ Peptide release of oxytocin, thyrotropin-releasing hormone or PACAP from a subset, by volume transmission.

◦ A scalar satiety signal usable by a simulation, expressed as spikes per second, range 0 to 12.

• STATE.

◦ Membrane voltage in millivolts.

◦ Open fraction of the Kir7.1 potassium channel, 0 to 1.

◦ Surface MC4R density, 0 to 1 of maximum.

◦ Slow adaptation variable tracking recent firing.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Reversal voltage with Kir7.1 shut, about minus 37 millivolts.

◦ Maximum hyperpolarisation with Kir7.1 fully open, about 25 millivolts.

◦ Spike threshold, about minus 45 millivolts.

◦ Input resistance, 500 megaohms to 1 gigaohm.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Membrane time constant, 20 to 60 milliseconds.

◦ Peptide receptor occupancy, 1 to 30 seconds.

◦ Synaptic rewiring by leptin, 2 to 24 hours.

◦ Receptor trafficking to the surface, hours to days.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Loss-of-function mutation in MC4R gives severe early-onset obesity, hyperphagia and increased lean mass, and is the commonest single-gene cause of human obesity at roughly 1 in 500 people.

◦ Loss of the receptor's partner protein melanocortin receptor accessory protein 2 gives a similar picture.

◦ Excess melanocortin drive, as in cancer-associated wasting, produces anorexia and loss of body mass.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to turn the tug of war between two arcuate peptides into the firing of a paraventricular melanocortin receptor 4 neuron, first in the fed state and then in the fasted state.
Create a number variable called kir_open_fraction with initial value 0.8.
Create a number variable called membrane_potential with initial value -62.
Create a number variable called depolarisation with initial value 0.
Create a boolean variable called firing with initial value false.
Create a list variable called peptide_balance with initial value empty list.
Add 0.9 to the end of peptide_balance.
Add -0.7 to the end of peptide_balance.
For each net_drive in peptide_balance, perform the following operations.
    Calculate the product of net_drive and 0.6.
    Set kir_open_fraction to the result.
    Calculate the difference of 0.8 and kir_open_fraction.
    Set kir_open_fraction to the result.
    Calculate the product of kir_open_fraction and 25.
    Set depolarisation to the result.
    Calculate the difference of -37 and depolarisation.
    Set membrane_potential to the result.
    Check if membrane_potential is greater than -45.
    If the result is true, then perform the following actions.
        Set firing to true.
    Otherwise, perform the following actions.
        Set firing to false.
    End the conditional block.
    Display membrane_potential to the console.
    Display firing to the console.
End the loop.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

# Python is chosen because this is a short numerical model with no dependencies,
# so a reader can paste it into any machine and watch satiety switch off.
ALPHA_FED = 1.0     # arcuate alpha-MSH tone, normalised 0 to 1
AGRP_FED = 0.1      # agouti-related peptide tone, normalised 0 to 1
E_KIR_SHUT = -37.0  # mV the cell drifts to when Kir7.1 is closed
KIR_SWING = 25.0    # mV of hyperpolarisation when Kir7.1 is fully open
THRESHOLD = -45.0   # mV spike threshold

def kir_open_fraction(alpha_msh, agrp):
    """alpha-MSH shuts the Kir7.1 potassium channel, AgRP re-opens it."""
    drive = alpha_msh - agrp
    frac = 0.8 - 0.6 * drive
    return min(1.0, max(0.0, frac))

def step(alpha_msh, agrp):
    frac = kir_open_fraction(alpha_msh, agrp)
    voltage = E_KIR_SHUT - KIR_SWING * frac
    return voltage, voltage > THRESHOLD

fed_spikes = 0
fasted_spikes = 0
for t in range(1000):
    if t < 500:
        a, g = ALPHA_FED, AGRP_FED          # fed: melanocortin tone high
    else:
        a, g = 0.2, 0.9                     # fasted: AgRP takes over
    voltage, fired = step(a, g)
    if fired and t < 500:
        fed_spikes += 1
    elif fired:
        fasted_spikes += 1
print("membrane voltage when fed:", round(step(ALPHA_FED, AGRP_FED)[0], 2))
print("membrane voltage when fasted:", round(step(0.2, 0.9)[0], 2))
print("spikes in the fed half:", fed_spikes)
print("spikes in the fasted half:", fasted_spikes)

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

It is the only neuron in the body whose excitability is set by a hormone receptor closing a potassium channel, whose resting signal is switched off rather than switched on by its rival peptide, and whose single-copy loss in a human being produces lifelong hunger and severe obesity with an otherwise entirely normal brain.

CHAPTER 103. OREXIN NEURONS, ALSO CALLED HYPOCRETIN NEURONS, OF THE LATERAL HYPOTHALAMUS (Sleep-Intrusion Blocking Neuron) (COG)

About seventy thousand cells, fewer than one in a million of your neurons, are the reason you can stay awake through a dull afternoon, and when the immune system destroys them a person begins collapsing into sleep in the middle of sentences.

Orexin and hypocretin are both peptide barcodes, and the fact that the same cell carries two competing chemical names shows how little either teaches.

Sleep-Intrusion names exactly what goes wrong without it, because when eighty-five to ninety-five percent of these cells are destroyed in narcolepsy type one, fragments of sleep invade the waking day as sudden sleep attacks and as cataplexy, the collapse of muscle tone during emotion.

Blocking names the work, since the cell does not create wakefulness so much as hold the boundary between states firm, exciting the histamine, noradrenaline and serotonin arousal centres so that transitions happen only at the right time.
